NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
TUESDAY, July 8, 2014 
 
PRESENT: Sylvia Allen, Supervisor; Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; David Tenney, Supervisor; Jonathan 
M. Nez, Supervisor; and Jesse Thompson, Chairman were present in person. 
9:05 a.m. Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Whitesinger. 
      CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Mary Springer addressed the Board. She indicated at 8:00 a.m. this morning, Navajo 
County, Apache County and the Apache Sitgreaves National F orest S ervice lifted  the fire restrictions. Mrs. 
Springer indicated because of the significant moisture received and with more moisture forecasted, this is enough 
to rescind the fire restrictions. 
CONSENT AGENDA: a) Minutes: June 24, 2014 b) Warrant and Voucher Report of $1000 June 2014 c)Juvenile 
Probation Report June 2014  d) Clerk of Superior Court Report June 2014  e) Justice Court Reports: Holbrook 
Precinct #1, June 2014: Winslow Precinct #2, June 2014; Snowflake Precinct #3, June 2014; Kayenta Precinct 
#4, June 2014; Show Low Precinct #5, June 2014; Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, June 2014 f) Constable Reports: 
Holbrook Precinct  #1, May 2014  g) Tax exemptions filed pursuant to A.R.S. §42 -11153 as a Request for 
Redemption of Waiver  h) Conduct board member elections for Heber Domestic Water Improvement District, 
Porter Creek Domestic Water Improvement District and Navapache Hospita l District on November 4, 2014  i) 
Conduct board member elections for Ponderosa Domestic Water Improvement District, Misty Mountain 
Domestic Water Improvement District, Porter Mountain Domestic Water Improvement District, Joseph City 
Sanitary District and O vergaard Townsite Domestic Water Improvement District on November 4, 2014  j) First 
Things First grant renewal in the amount of $60,0000 for Parent Outreach in Navajo and Apache counties  k) 
Arizona Criminal Justice Commission Byrne grant #DC-15-009 for $241,425 for operation of the Major Crimes 
Apprehension Team l) Community Development Block Grant contract change order number 001 for Snowflake 
Academy Project #155 -12 m) Fourteenth amendment to the Revolving Credit Agreement and request for 
Modification of Commitment amount for Joseph City Fire, White Mountain Lake Fire and Show Low Fire  n) 
Application for extension of premises/patio permit for Wild Women Saloon and Grill located at 2269 Hwy 260, 
Overgaard for the Arizona Crawfish Festival on August 2, 20 14 o) Amendment Number One to a Professional 
Services Contract with Stantec Consulting Services, Inc. For the Public Works Complex Well, Storage and Pump 
Station Facilities p) Amendment Number Three to the Professional Services Agreement with Johnson Walze r 
Associates, LLC to provide Architectural/Engineering services for the Navajo County Public Works Complex for 
an amount of $7,270.00: Supervisor Tenney made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the 
consent agenda as presented; motion se conded by Supervisor Whitesinger . Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. 
WE ARE NAVAJO COUNTY : Navajo County 4 th of July Parade Float : Homero Vela , Assistant County 
Manager and Sheila Malone with Public Works discussed  the video presentation. Mr. Vela thanked the float 
decorating teams and all of those who assisted with the Show Low and the Heber-Overgaard parades. 
BOARD OF EQUALIZATION: 
a. Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners: Catrina Roe swore in Cammy Darris, Assessor. 
b. Discussion and possible action regarding Petition for Review of Taxpayer Notice of Claim APN 207-12-
078B: Cammy Darris discussed the item. Supervisor Tenney made a motion to approve the Petition for

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 07-08-2014 
    Page 2 of 3 
 
Review of Taxpayer Notice of Claim APN 207 -12-078B; No Change to Full Cash or Limited Values; 
2010-2011- Improvement and 10 Acres of Land Value to Class 03 (owner occupied) Remaining acreage 
to Class 02; 2012 -Current- Improvement and 10 Acres of Land V alue to Class 0401  (non-primary 
Residence) Remaining acreage to Class 02 . The m otion was seconded by  Supervisor Allen. Vote  
unanimous approving the motion.                                                
 
HUMAN RESOURCES: Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions : Kimberly Eavenson  
presented the 26 item  Personnel Action R eport. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to approve the Personnel 
Action Report as presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Nez. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
COUNTY ATTORNEY: 
a. Consideration and possible approval of the 2014/2015 Victims of Crime Act (VOCA) Grant award: Lynda 
Young presented the item. Supervisor Nez made a motion to approve the 2014/2015 Victims of Crime 
Act (VOCA) Grant award; motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of the Arizona Criminal Justice Commission Victim Compensation 
Grant #VC-15-058 award: Lynda Young presented the item. Supervisor Nez made a motion to approve 
the Arizona Criminal Justice Commi ssion Victim Compensation Grant #VC-15-058 award; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
FINANCE: Consideration and possible approval of 155 -12 Community Development Block Grant Snowflake 
Academy Amendment #2: Lynda Young presented the item. Supervisor Nez made a motion to approve the 155-
12 Community Development Block Grant Snowflake Academy Amendment #2; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
PUBLIC WORKS: Consideration and possible approval for Public Works to sign a Change Order for the Public 
Works Complex contract, #B13-06-02 with Haydon Building Corporation expanding the scope of work to include 
utility infrastructure and concrete work: Homero Vela presented the item. Supervisor Allen made a motion to 
approve for Public Works to sign a Change Order for the Public Works Complex contract, #B13 -06-02 with 
Haydon Building Corporation expanding the scope of work to include utility infrastructure and concrete work; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Tenney. Vote unanimous approving the motion.    
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE : Report from Board members, County Attorney and Assistant County 
Manager: Supervisor Nez reported on the July 4th event in Kayenta. He indicated that the event was attended by 
Judge Nelson and some of the Navajo County Sheriff’ s deputies. He expressed appreciation to the C ounty for 
their assistance. Supervisor Nez stated the Navajo Rodeo Historical Society , which is a non-profit entity, had  
about 50 people who attended their kick-off meeting. Supervisor Nez suggested that a fire district be formed in 
the Kayenta/Pinon area, should be considered by the Board, with a percentage of the funding coming from gaming 
revenues. Supervisor Nez indicated the Red Dog Gravel project is fully funded for the next batch, with the Navajo 
Nation committing funds to the pro ject as well as funds from the C ounty. He suggested an extension for  the 
graveling project should be for the life of the Peabody Lease of 25 years . Supervisor Nez indicated a Public 
Safety Dedication Grand Opening will be happening soon for the Justice Center in  Kayenta. He stated the top 
three justice centers being proposed are all within Navajo County; Kayenta, Pinon and the Dilkon Justice Centers, 
which are coming soon. He expressed appreciation to Sheriff K.C. Clark and Brad Carlyon, County Attorney for 
their assistance with the justice centers. Supervisor Nez reported he ran and completed  his second,  50 mile 
marathon in Moab, Utah. Supervisor Allen spoke about the 4 th of July parade that she was able to attend.  She 
indicated she a ttended the Apache and  Navajo Counties Mayors and  Councilmembers Association meeting.

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 07-08-2014 
    Page 3 of 3 
 
Supervisor Allen remarked on the ribbon cutting for the High Desert Health Center opening and how impressed 
she was with the facility.  Supervisor Allen stated she had a meeting with a group of legislators  and different 
county supervisors  regarding the Transfer of Public Lands . She st ated she attended the Snowflake -Taylor 
Chamber of Commerce dinner and auction. She reported on the AACo conference and the governor’s debate and 
also met with District 6 legislators. Supervisor Whitesinger indicated she is working with the White Mountain 
Apache Tribe and the new Tribal Council members to create a sub-committee on alcoholism. She stated they will 
work together with local community organizations to form a plan to present to the Tribal C ouncil at the end of 
September. Supervisor Whitesinger  stated the WMAT T ribe is having an upcoming Youth Leadershi p 
Conference regarding higher education , with all the surrounding schools invited . She indicated the Safe House 
Board is having a Bingo fundraiser this Saturday at the Hampton Inn. She reported she visited with several cattle 
owners in the Tribal area to d iscuss the issues of land use, grazing, getting loans for e quipment and the farming 
of alfalfa. Supervisor Tenney reported he went to the AACo C onference. He stated he will attend the NACo 
conference in New Orleans where there will be discussions of P ayment in Lieu of Taxes, (PILT). Homero Vela 
spoke about the various 4 th of July celebrations and the opportunity for the communities to come together.  Mr. 
Vela discussed t he High D esert Health Care Facility Grand O pening and spoke of the implementation of the 
Health Care Plan.  Chairman Thompson reported he attended the AACo C onference. He indicated he made a 
presentation to  the Chinle community regarding  the faith based organizations, “American Indian Religious 
Freedom Act”. Chairman Thompson spoke of the success of the grand opening of the High Desert Health Care 
Facility. Chairman Thompson indicated he, James Jayne and Homero Vela met with the Winslow Rotary and 
presented the County’s budget. He stated he will attend the NACo Conference in New Orleans. 
ADJOURN: At 10:04 a.m. Supervisor Tenney made a motion  to adjourn; motion seconded by  Supervisor 
Allen. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
 
 
APPROVED: 
 
Jesse Thompson, Chairman 
 
ATTEST:  
 
 
 
 
DATE:  July 22, 2014 
 
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board
